1.16Sheet 1

(a) Find the 16's complement of B2FA. (b) Convert B2FA to binary. (c) Find the 2's complement of the result in (b). (d) Convert the answer in (c) to hexadecimal and compare with (a).

Answer: (a) 4D06 (b) 1011 0010 1111 1010 (c) 0100 1101 0000 0110 (d) 4D06, the same

Solution on paper

(a) 15's complement: subtract each digit from F → 4D05; add 1 → 4D06.

each digit -> 4 bits:
   B    2    F    A  
  1011 0010 1111 1010

(c) 1's complement 0100110100000101, plus 1 → 0100110100000110.

groups of 4 bits (zeros added at the ends):
  0100 1101 0000 0110
   4    D    0    6

The 16's complement in hex and the 2's complement in binary are the same operation.

Sheet 1 · problems 1.17 to 1.23

1.17Sheet 1

Perform subtraction on the given unsigned numbers using the 10's complement of the subtrahend. Where the result should be negative, find its 10's complement and affix a minus sign. (a) 6,428 − 3,409 (b) 125 − 1,800 (c) 2,043 − 6,152 (d) 1,631 − 745

Answer: (a) 3019 (b) −1675 (c) −4109 (d) 886

Solution on paper

(a) 6,428 − 3,409 = 3019

                     M =   6428
  10's complement of N = + 6591
                         ------
                   Sum =  13019
     discard end carry   -10000
                Answer =   3019

(b) 125 − 1,800 = −1675

                     M =   0125
  10's complement of N = + 8200
                         ------
                   Sum =   8325
no end carry -> answer is negative:
  -(10's complement of 8325) = -1675

(c) 2,043 − 6,152 = −4109

                     M =   2043
  10's complement of N = + 3848
                         ------
                   Sum =   5891
no end carry -> answer is negative:
  -(10's complement of 5891) = -4109

(d) 1,631 − 745 = 886

                     M =   1631
  10's complement of N = + 9255
                         ------
                   Sum =  10886
     discard end carry   -10000
                Answer =   0886

In (d) the handwritten solution takes the 10's complement of 745 with three digits (255) and then subtracts \(10^3\). The answer 886 is right, but the method on the slides pads both numbers to the same length first: 0745 → 9255.

1.19Sheet 1

+9,286 and +801 are given in sign-magnitude form. Convert them to signed-10's-complement form and perform: (a) (+9,286) + (+801) (b) (+9,286) + (−801) (c) (−9,286) + (+801) (d) (−9,286) + (−801). The sum +10,087 needs five digits and a sign.

Answer: (a) +10087 (b) +8485 (c) −8485 (d) −10087

Solution on paper

Use one sign digit (0 = plus, 9 = minus) and five digits. \(+9286=0\,09286\), \(+801=0\,00801\). A negative number is the 10's complement of the whole 6-digit number: \(-9286=9\,90714\), \(-801=9\,99199\).

(a) (+9286) + (+801)
   009286
 + 000801
   ------
   010087   -> sign digit 0: +10087
(b) (+9286) + (-801)
   009286
 + 999199
   ------
  1008485   -> sign digit 0: +8485
   (end carry discarded)
(c) (-9286) + (+801)
   990714
 + 000801
   ------
   991515   -> sign digit 9: negative, 10's complement of 991515 = 008485 -> -8485
(d) (-9286) + (-801)
   990714
 + 999199
   ------
  1989913   -> sign digit 9: negative, 10's complement of 989913 = 010087 -> -10087
   (end carry discarded)

The handwritten solution uses only five digits in total (09286, 99199 …). Its results are right, but with five digits the sum in (a) and (d) does not fit (10087 would start with the digit 1, which is not a sign), so six digits are needed, as the question says.

1.20Sheet 1

Convert decimal +46 and +29 to binary, using the signed-2's-complement representation and enough digits to accommodate the numbers. Then perform (+29) + (−49), (−29) + (+49) and (−29) + (−49). Convert the answers back to decimal and verify.

Answer: −20, +20, −78 (8 bits are needed)

Solution on paper
Number8-bit signed-2's complement
+4600101110
+2900011101
+4900110001
−2911100011
−4911001111
  00011101   (+29)
+ 11001111   (-49)
  --------
  11101100   (-20)
  11100011   (-29)
+ 00110001   (+49)
  --------
1 00010100   (+20)
^ carry out of the sign bit is discarded
  11100011   (-29)
+ 11001111   (-49)
  --------
1 10110010   (-78)
^ carry out of the sign bit is discarded

The question names +46 but then uses 49 in the sums; this is how it is printed in the textbook. The handwritten solution starts with 7 bits (range −64 to +63), meets an overflow in (−29) + (−49) = −78, and repeats that sum with 8 bits. Using 8 bits from the start avoids the problem: the range is −128 to +127.

1.23Sheet 1

Represent the unsigned decimal numbers 842 and 537 in BCD, and then show the steps necessary to form their sum.

Answer: \(842+537=1379=(0001\ 0011\ 0111\ 1001)_{BCD}\)

Solution on paper
carry in                               
842                  1000   0100   0010
+ 537                0101   0011   0111
                    -----  -----  -----
binary sum           1101   0111   1001
add 6 if > 9        +0110              
BCD sum          1   0011   0111   1001
decimal          1      3      7      9

Digits whose binary sum is above 1001 (or that produce a carry) get +0110 and send a carry of 1 to the next digit on the left.

Only the hundreds digit (8 + 5 = 13 = 1101) is above 9; it gets +0110 and sends a carry that becomes the thousands digit.

1.31Sheet 1

How many printing characters are there in ASCII? How many of them are special characters (not letters or numerals)?

Answer: 94 printing characters; 32 special characters

Solution on paper
\[\begin{aligned}\text{printing} &= 128-34 \\[3pt] &= \boxed{94}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}\text{special} &= 94-26-26-10 \\[3pt] &= \boxed{32}\end{aligned}\]

26 capital letters, 26 small letters and 10 numerals leave 32 special characters such as ! " # $ % &. The handwritten solution writes 34 for the second answer; 34 is the number of non-printing characters, not of special printing characters.

1.32Sheet 1

What bit must be complemented to change an ASCII letter from capital to lowercase and vice versa?

Answer: bit 6, the sixth bit from the right (weight 32)

Solution on paper

A = 1000001 and a = 1100001. They differ only in the bit of weight \(2^5=32\), which the lecture calls bit 6.

2.2Sheet 2

Simplify the following Boolean expressions to a minimum number of literals: (a) \(xy+xy'\) (b) \((x+y)(x+y')\) (c) \(xyz+x'y+xyz'\) (d) \((A+B)'(A'+B')'\) (e) \(xyz'+x'yz+xyz+x'yz'\) (f) \((x+y+z')(x'+y'+z)\)

Answer: (a) \(x\) (b) \(x\) (c) \(y\) (d) 0 (e) \(y\) (f) \(xy'+x'z'+yz\) (6 literals, the same count as the given form)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}xy+xy' &= x(y+y') && \text{factor} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x} && \text{\(y+y'=1\)}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}(x+y)(x+y') &= x+yy' && \text{\((a+b)(a+c)=a+bc\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x} && \text{\(yy'=0\)}\end{aligned}\]

(c)

\[\begin{aligned}xyz+x'y+xyz' &= xy(z+z')+x'y \\[3pt] &= xy+x'y \\[3pt] &= y(x+x') \\[3pt] &= \boxed{y}\end{aligned}\]

(d)

\[\begin{aligned}(A+B)'(A'+B')' &= (A'B')(AB) && \text{DeMorgan twice} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{0} && \text{\(AA'=0\)}\end{aligned}\]

(e)

\[\begin{aligned}xyz'+x'yz+xyz+x'yz' &= xy(z'+z)+x'y(z+z') \\[3pt] &= xy+x'y \\[3pt] &= \boxed{y}\end{aligned}\]

(f)

\[\begin{aligned}(x+y+z')(x'+y'+z) &= xy'+xz+x'y+yz+x'z'+y'z' && \text{multiply out, \(xx'=yy'=zz'=0\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{xy'+x'z'+yz} && \text{the other three terms are consensus terms}\end{aligned}\]

In (f) the sum of products \(xy'+x'z'+yz\) (or equally \(x'y+xz+y'z'\)) has six literals, the same as the product of sums that was given; the function cannot be written with fewer.

2.3Sheet 2

Simplify to a minimum number of literals: (a) \(ABC+A'B+ABC'\) (b) \(x'yz+xz\) (c) \((x+y)'(x'+y')\) (d) \(xy+x(wz+wz')\) (e) \((BC'+A'D)(AB'+CD')\) (f) \((x+y'+z')(x'+z')\)

Answer: (a) \(B\) (b) \(z(x+y)\) (c) \(x'y'\) (d) \(x(w+y)\) (e) 0 (f) \(z'+x'y'\)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}ABC+A'B+ABC' &= AB(C+C')+A'B \\[3pt] &= AB+A'B \\[3pt] &= \boxed{B}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}x'yz+xz &= z(x'y+x) \\[3pt] &= \boxed{z(x+y)} && \text{\(x+x'y=x+y\)}\end{aligned}\]

(c)

\[\begin{aligned}(x+y)'(x'+y') &= x'y'(x'+y') && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= x'y'+x'y' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x'y'}\end{aligned}\]

(d)

\[\begin{aligned}xy+x(wz+wz') &= xy+xw(z+z') \\[3pt] &= xy+xw \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x(y+w)}\end{aligned}\]

(e)

\[\begin{aligned}(BC'+A'D)(AB'+CD') &= ABB'C'+BC'CD'+AA'B'D+A'CDD' && \text{multiply out} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{0} && \text{every term holds a variable and its complement}\end{aligned}\]

(f)

\[\begin{aligned}(x+y'+z')(x'+z') &= z'+(x+y')x' && \text{\((a+b)(a+c)=a+bc\) with \(a=z'\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{z'+x'y'} && \text{\(xx'=0\)}\end{aligned}\]
2.4Sheet 2

Reduce the following Boolean expressions to the indicated number of literals: (a) \(A'C'+ABC+AC'\) to three (b) \((x'y'+z)'+z+xy+wz\) to three (c) \(A'B(D'+C'D)+B(A+A'CD)\) to one (d) \((A'+C)(A'+C')(A+B+C'D)\) to four (e) \(ABCD+A'BD+ABC'D\) to two

Answer: (a) \(AB+C'\) (b) \(x+y+z\) (c) \(B\) (d) \(A'(B+C'D)\) (e) \(BD\)

Solution on paper

(a) to three literals

\[\begin{aligned}A'C'+ABC+AC' &= C'(A'+A)+ABC \\[3pt] &= C'+ABC \\[3pt] &= (C'+C)(C'+AB) && \text{\(a+bc=(a+b)(a+c)\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{C'+AB}\end{aligned}\]

(b) to three literals

\[\begin{aligned}(x'y'+z)'+z+xy+wz &= (x+y)z'+z+xy+wz && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= (x+y)z'+z+xy && \text{\(z+wz=z\)} \\[3pt] &= x+y+z+xy && \text{\(z+z'a=z+a\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x+y+z} && \text{\(x+xy=x\)}\end{aligned}\]

(c) to one literal

\[\begin{aligned}A'B(D'+C'D)+B(A+A'CD) &= B(A'D'+A'C'D+A+A'CD) && \text{factor B} \\[3pt] &= B(A'D'+A'D(C'+C)+A) \\[3pt] &= B(A'(D'+D)+A) \\[3pt] &= B(A'+A) \\[3pt] &= \boxed{B}\end{aligned}\]

(d) to four literals

\[\begin{aligned}(A'+C)(A'+C')(A+B+C'D) &= (A'+CC')(A+B+C'D) \\[3pt] &= A'(A+B+C'D) \\[3pt] &= A'B+A'C'D && \text{\(A'A=0\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'(B+C'D)}\end{aligned}\]

(e) to two literals

\[\begin{aligned}ABCD+A'BD+ABC'D &= ABD(C+C')+A'BD \\[3pt] &= ABD+A'BD \\[3pt] &= BD(A+A') \\[3pt] &= \boxed{BD}\end{aligned}\]

2.8Sheet 2

Find the complement of \(F=wx+yz\); then show that \(FF'=0\) and \(F+F'=1\).

Answer: \(F'=(w'+x')(y'+z')\)

Solution on paper
\[\begin{aligned}F' &= (wx+yz)' \\[3pt] &= (wx)'(yz)' && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(w'+x')(y'+z')} && \text{DeMorgan}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}FF' &= (wx+yz)(w'+x')(y'+z') \\[3pt] &= wx(w'+x')(y'+z')+yz(w'+x')(y'+z') \\[3pt] &= (wxw'+wxx')(y'+z')+(yzy'+yzz')(w'+x') \\[3pt] &= 0+0 \\[3pt] &= \boxed{0}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}F+F' &= wx+yz+(wx+yz)' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{1} && \text{\(a+a'=1\) with \(a=wx+yz\)}\end{aligned}\]
2.9Sheet 2

Find the complement of the following expressions: (a) \(xy'+x'y\) (b) \((A'B+CD)E'+E\) (c) \((x'+y+z')(x+y')(x+z)\)

Answer: (a) \(xy+x'y'\) (b) \((A+B')(C'+D')E'\) (c) \(xy'z+x'y+x'z'\)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}(xy'+x'y)' &= (x'+y)(x+y') && \text{dual, then complement each literal} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{xy+x'y'} && \text{multiply out}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}((A'B+CD)E'+E)' &= (A'B+CD+E)' && \text{\(E+E'a=E+a\)} \\[3pt] &= (A'B)'(CD)'E' && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(A+B')(C'+D')E'}\end{aligned}\]

(c)

\[\begin{aligned}((x'+y+z')(x+y')(x+z))' &= (x'+y+z')'+(x+y')'+(x+z)' && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{xy'z+x'y+x'z'}\end{aligned}\]
2.10Sheet 2

Given the Boolean functions \(F_1\) and \(F_2\), show that (a) \(E=F_1+F_2\) contains the sum of the minterms of \(F_1\) and \(F_2\); (b) \(G=F_1F_2\) contains only the minterms that are common to \(F_1\) and \(F_2\).

Answer: shown below

Solution on paper

Write \(F_1=\Sigma m_i\) and \(F_2=\Sigma m_j\).

(a) \(E=F_1+F_2=\Sigma m_i+\Sigma m_j\). A minterm that appears in both lists is written once, because \(m+m=m\). So E is the OR of every minterm found in \(F_1\) or in \(F_2\).

(b) \(G=F_1F_2=(\Sigma m_i)(\Sigma m_j)\). Multiplying out gives products \(m_im_j\). Two different minterms differ in at least one variable, which is primed in one and unprimed in the other, so \(m_im_j=0\) when \(i\ne j\), and \(m_im_i=m_i\). Only the minterms present in both functions survive.

Example: \(F_1=\Sigma(1,3,5)\), \(F_2=\Sigma(3,4,5)\) → \(E=\Sigma(1,3,4,5)\), \(G=\Sigma(3,5)\).

We can perform logical operations on strings of bits by considering each pair of corresponding bits separately (bitwise operation). Given A = 10110001 and B = 10101100, evaluate the eight-bit result after: (a) AND (b) OR (c) XOR (d) NOT A (e) NOT B.

OperationResult
A10110001
B10101100
(a) A AND B10100000
(b) A OR B10111101
(c) A XOR B00011101
(d) NOT A01001110
(e) NOT B01010011

Work column by column: AND gives 1 only for 1 and 1; OR gives 0 only for 0 and 0; XOR gives 1 where the two bits differ.

2.14Sheet 2

Implement the Boolean function \(F=xy+x'y'+y'z\) (a) with AND, OR, and inverter gates, (b) with OR and inverter gates, (c) with AND and inverter gates, (d) with NAND and inverter gates, and (e) with NOR and inverter gates.

Answer: five circuits, all checked equal to F

Solution on paper

(a) Directly: three AND gates and one OR gate.

(b) Replace each AND by OR with DeMorgan: \(xy=(x'+y')'\), \(x'y'=(x+y)'\), \(y'z=(y+z')'\). So \(F=(x'+y')'+(x+y)'+(y+z')'\).

(c) Replace the OR by AND: \(F=[(xy)'\,(x'y')'\,(y'z)']'\).

(d) The same expression, each “AND then NOT” drawn as one NAND gate: two-level NAND–NAND.

(e) From (b): each “OR then NOT” is a NOR gate, \(F=\{[(x'+y')'+(x+y)'+(y+z')']'\}'\): three NOR gates, one collecting NOR, and an inverter at the output.

2.15Sheet 2

Simplify the Boolean functions \(T_1\) and \(T_2\) to a minimum number of literals. From the table: \(T_1=1\) for ABC = 000, 001, 010 and \(T_2=1\) for 011, 100, 101, 110, 111.

Answer: \(T_1=A'(B'+C')\), \(T_2=A+BC=T_1'\)

Solution on paper
\(A\)\(B\)\(C\)\(T_1\)\(T_2\)

\[\begin{aligned}T_1 &= A'B'(C'+C)+A'C'(B'+B) && \text{use \(A'B'C'\) twice} \\[3pt] &= A'B'+A'C' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'(B'+C')}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}T_2 &= T_1' && \text{the two columns are complements} \\[3pt] &= [A'(B'+C')]' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A+BC} && \text{DeMorgan}\end{aligned}\]
2.16Sheet 2

The logical sum of all minterms of a Boolean function of n variables is 1. (a) Prove the previous statement for n = 3. (b) Suggest a procedure for a general proof.

Answer: proved below

Solution on paper
\[\begin{aligned}\Sigma m_i &= x'y'(z'+z)+x'y(z'+z)+xy'(z'+z)+xy(z'+z) && \text{group in pairs} \\[3pt] &= x'y'+x'y+xy'+xy && \text{\(z+z'=1\)} \\[3pt] &= x'(y'+y)+x(y'+y) \\[3pt] &= x'+x \\[3pt] &= \boxed{1}\end{aligned}\]

(b) General proof by induction: the \(2^{\,n}\) minterms of n variables split into two halves, those with \(x_n'\) and those with \(x_n\). Factoring gives (sum of all minterms of \(n-1\) variables)·\((x_n'+x_n)\) = (sum for \(n-1\) variables)·1. Repeating down to one variable leaves \(x_1'+x_1=1\). In words: for any input combination exactly one minterm is 1, so their OR is always 1.

2.19Sheet 2

Express the following function as a sum of minterms and as a product of maxterms: \(F(A,B,C,D)=B'D+A'D+BD\).

Answer: \(F=D=\Sigma(1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15)=\Pi(0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14)\)

Solution on paper
\[\begin{aligned}B'D+A'D+BD &= D(B'+B)+A'D \\[3pt] &= D+A'D \\[3pt] &= \boxed{D} && \text{absorption}\end{aligned}\]

\(F=D\): the function is 1 in every row where D = 1, the odd-numbered rows, and 0 in the even-numbered rows.

\[F=\boxed{\Sigma(1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15)=\Pi(0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14)}\]
2.20Sheet 2

Express the complement of the following functions in sum-of-minterms form: (a) \(F(A,B,C,D)=\Sigma(3,5,9,11,15)\) (b) \(F(x,y,z)=\Pi(2,4,5,7)\)

Answer: (a) \(F'=\Sigma(0, 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 13, 14)\) (b) \(F'=\Sigma(2,4,5,7)\)

Solution on paper

(a) The complement is 1 where F is 0: list the missing numbers from 0 to 15: \(F'=\Sigma(0, 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 13, 14)\).

(b) \(F=\Pi(2,4,5,7)\) says F is 0 at rows 2, 4, 5, 7. So \(F'\) is 1 exactly there: \(F'=\Sigma(2,4,5,7)\). Same numbers, other symbol.

2.21Sheet 2

Convert each of the following to the other canonical form: (a) \(F(x,y,z)=\Sigma(2,5,6)\) (b) \(F(A,B,C,D)=\Pi(0,1,2,4,7,9,12)\)

Answer: (a) \(\Pi(0,1,3,4,7)\) (b) \(\Sigma(3, 5, 6, 8, 10, 11, 13, 14, 15)\)

Solution on paper

Swap the symbol and list the missing numbers.

(a) 3 variables, rows 0 to 7; missing from {2, 5, 6}: 0, 1, 3, 4, 7 → \(F=\Pi(0,1,3,4,7)\).

(b) 4 variables, rows 0 to 15; missing from {0, 1, 2, 4, 7, 9, 12}: \(F=\Sigma(3, 5, 6, 8, 10, 11, 13, 14, 15)\).

2.22Sheet 2

Convert each of the following expressions into sum of products and product of sums: (a) \((AB+C)(B+C'D)\) (b) \(x'+x(x+y')(y+z')\)

Answer: (a) SOP: \(AB+BC\); POS: \(B(A+C)\) (b) \(x'+y+z'\), which is both a sum of products and a single sum term

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}(AB+C)(B+C'D) &= AB+ABC'D+BC+CC'D && \text{multiply out} \\[3pt] &= AB+ABC'D+BC && \text{\(CC'=0\)} \\[3pt] &= AB+BC && \text{\(AB+ABC'D=AB\): sum of products} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{B(A+C)} && \text{factor: product of sums}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}x'+x(x+y')(y+z') &= x'+(x+y')(y+z') && \text{\(a'+ab=a'+b\)} \\[3pt] &= x'+xy+xz'+y'z' && \text{multiply out, \(yy'=0\)} \\[3pt] &= x'+y+z'+y'z' && \text{\(x'+xy=x'+y\), \(x'+xz'=x'+z'\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{x'+y+z'} && \text{\(z'+y'z'=z'\)}\end{aligned}\]

\(x'+y+z'\) is a sum of three one-literal products, and it is also one sum term, the maxterm \(M_5\): \(F=\Pi(5)\).

2.24Sheet 2

Show that the dual of the exclusive-OR is equal to its complement.

Answer: dual of \(xy'+x'y\) is \((x+y')(x'+y)=xy+x'y'=(x\oplus y)'\)

Solution on paper
\[\begin{aligned}x\oplus y &= xy'+x'y\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}\text{dual} &= (x+y')(x'+y) && \text{swap + and ·} \\[3pt] &= xx'+xy+x'y'+yy' && \text{multiply out} \\[3pt] &= xy+x'y' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(x\oplus y)'} && \text{this is the XNOR}\end{aligned}\]
2.25Sheet 2

By substituting the Boolean expression equivalent of the binary operations as defined in Table 2.8, show the following: (a) the inhibition operation is neither commutative nor associative; (b) the exclusive-OR operation is commutative and associative.

Answer: shown below

Solution on paper

(a) Inhibition: \(x/y=xy'\).

\[\begin{aligned}x/y &= xy'\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}y/x &= yx' && \text{different from \(xy'\): not commutative}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}(x/y)/z &= (xy')z'=xy'z'\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}x/(y/z) &= x(yz')'=x(y'+z)=xy'+xz && \text{different: not associative}\end{aligned}\]

(b) Exclusive-OR: \(x\oplus y=xy'+x'y\).

\[\begin{aligned}y\oplus x &= yx'+y'x=xy'+x'y=x\oplus y && \text{commutative}\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}(x\oplus y)\oplus z &= (xy'+x'y)z'+(xy'+x'y)'z \\[3pt] &= xy'z'+x'yz'+(xy+x'y')z \\[3pt] &= xy'z'+x'yz'+xyz+x'y'z\end{aligned}\]
\[\begin{aligned}x\oplus(y\oplus z) &= x(yz'+y'z)'+x'(yz'+y'z) \\[3pt] &= x(yz+y'z')+x'yz'+x'y'z \\[3pt] &= xyz+xy'z'+x'yz'+x'y'z && \text{the same four minterms: associative}\end{aligned}\]
2.26Sheet 2

Show that a positive logic NAND gate is a negative logic NOR gate and vice versa.

Answer: shown with the H / L table

Solution on paper
xyzpositive logic (H = 1)negative logic (H = 0)
LLH0 0 → 11 1 → 0
LHH0 1 → 11 0 → 0
HLH1 0 → 10 1 → 0
HHL1 1 → 00 0 → 1

The same hardware table read with H = 1 is 0 only for inputs 1 1: a NAND. Read with H = 0 it is 1 only for inputs 0 0: a NOR. Starting from the table of a positive-logic NOR and reading it in negative logic gives a NAND in the same way. Algebraically, swapping 0 and 1 everywhere turns \((xy)'\) into its dual \((x+y)'\).

2.27Sheet 2

Write the Boolean equations and draw the logic diagram of the circuit whose outputs are defined by the truth table (inputs a, b, c; outputs \(f_1\), \(f_2\)).

Answer: \(f_1=a'b'c' + bc + ab\), \(f_2=a'b + ab' + bc'\)

Solution on paper
\(a\)\(b\)\(c\)\(f_1\)\(f_2\)
00010

10101
11011
11110
\[f_1=\Sigma(0,3,6,7)=a'b'c'+a'bc+abc'+abc,\qquad f_2=\Sigma(2,3,4,5,6)\]
\[f_1=\boxed{a'b'c' + bc + ab}\qquad f_2=\boxed{a'b + ab' + bc'}\]

In the sheet the output columns are printed on the left of the input columns. For \(f_2\) there is a second equally short answer: the 1 in square 6 can be grouped with square 4 (\(ac'\)) or with square 2 (\(bc'\)).

2.28Sheet 2

Write Boolean expressions and construct the truth tables describing the outputs of the circuits described by the logic diagrams. (a) b, c, d feed a NAND gate; its output, a and e feed a second NAND gate, followed by an inverter (output y). (b) c, d, e feed an OR gate; a and that OR output feed an XOR gate (output \(y_1\)); the inverted b, the OR output and f feed a NAND gate followed by an inverter (output \(y_2\)).

Answer: (a) \(y=a(bcd)'e\) (b) \(y_1=a\oplus(c+d+e)\), \(y_2=b'(c+d+e)f\)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}y &= \{[a\,(bcd)'\,e]'\}' && \text{NAND then inverter} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{a\,(bcd)'\,e=a(b'+c'+d')e}\end{aligned}\]

Truth table in short form (32 rows): y = 1 only when \(a=1\), \(e=1\) and b, c, d are not all 1. These are the 7 rows abcde = 10001, 10011, 10101, 10111, 11001, 11011, 11101 (minterms 17, 19, 21, 23, 25, 27, 29). In the other 25 rows y = 0.

(b) Let \(s=c+d+e\) (s = 0 only when c = d = e = 0).

\[y_1=\boxed{a\oplus(c+d+e)}\qquad y_2=\boxed{b'(c+d+e)f}\]
as = c+d+e\(y_1=a\oplus s\)
000
011
101
110
bs = c+d+ef\(y_2=b'sf\)
0000
0010
0100
0111
1000
1010
1100
1110

The full table has 64 rows; the two small tables give every row, because c, d, e enter only through s. \(y_1\) has 32 minterms and \(y_2\) has 14.

Lecture 6 report sheet (Boolean algebra exercises)

The sheet is written with overbars. Below, a prime means the same as a bar: \(A'=\overline A\).

Report 1Lec 6 report sheet

Apply DeMorgan's theorems to each expression: (a) \(\overline{A\overline B(C+\overline D)}\) (b) \(\overline{AB(CD+EF)}\) (c) \(\overline{(A+\overline B+C+\overline D)}+\overline{ABC\overline D}\) (d) \(\overline{\overline{(\overline A+B+C+D)}\;\overline{(A\overline B\,\overline CD)}}\)

Answer: (a) \(\overline A+B+\overline CD\) (b) \(\overline A+\overline B+(\overline C+\overline D)(\overline E+\overline F)\) (c) \(\overline A+\overline B+\overline C+D\) (d) \(\overline A+B+C+D\)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}\overline{A\overline B(C+\overline D)} &= A'+B+(C+D')' && \text{bar over a product → sum of the complements} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'+B+C'D} && \text{DeMorgan on the last term}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}\overline{AB(CD+EF)} &= A'+B'+(CD+EF)' \\[3pt] &= A'+B'+(CD)'(EF)' \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'+B'+(C'+D')(E'+F')}\end{aligned}\]

(c)

\[\begin{aligned}\overline{(A+\overline B+C+\overline D)}+\overline{ABC\overline D} &= A'BC'D+A'+B'+C'+D && \text{DeMorgan on each bar} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'+B'+C'+D} && \text{\(A'+A'BC'D=A'\)}\end{aligned}\]

(d)

\[\begin{aligned}\overline{\overline{(\overline A+B+C+D)}\;\overline{(A\overline B\,\overline CD)}} &= (A'+B+C+D)+AB'C'D && \text{bar over a product; the double bars cancel} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'+B+C+D} && \text{\(A'+AB'C'D=A'+B'C'D\), then \(B+B'C'D=B+C'D\), then \(C+C'D=C+D\)}\end{aligned}\]
Report 2Lec 6 report sheet

Simplify with the rules of Boolean algebra: (a) \(F=A\overline B+A\overline{(B+C)}+B\overline{(B+C)}\) (b) \(F=[AB(C+\overline{BD})+\overline{AB}]CD\) (c) \(F=AB\overline C+\overline A\,\overline BC+\overline ABC+\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C\) (d) \(F=\overline{AB}+\overline{AC}+\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C\)

Answer: (a) \(A\overline B\) (b) \(CD\) (c) \(\overline A\,\overline B+\overline AC+AB\overline C\) (d) \(\overline A+\overline B+\overline C=\overline{ABC}\)

Solution on paper

(a)

\[\begin{aligned}AB'+A(B+C)'+B(B+C)' &= AB'+AB'C'+BB'C' && \text{DeMorgan: \((B+C)'=B'C'\)} \\[3pt] &= AB'+AB'C' && \text{\(BB'=0\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{AB'} && \text{absorption}\end{aligned}\]

(b)

\[\begin{aligned}(AB(C+(BD)')+(AB)')CD &= (AB(C+B'+D')+(AB)')CD && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= ((AB)'+C+B'+D')CD && \text{\(a'+ab=a'+b\) with \(a=AB\)} \\[3pt] &= (A'+B'+C+D')CD && \text{\((AB)'=A'+B'\)} \\[3pt] &= A'CD+B'CD+CD+0 && \text{\(D'D=0\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{CD} && \text{absorption}\end{aligned}\]

(c)

\[\begin{aligned}ABC'+A'B'C+A'BC+A'B'C' &= ABC'+A'B'(C+C')+A'BC \\[3pt] &= ABC'+A'B'+A'BC \\[3pt] &= ABC'+A'(B'+BC) \\[3pt] &= ABC'+A'(B'+C) && \text{\(B'+BC=B'+C\)} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'B'+A'C+ABC'}\end{aligned}\]

(d)

\[\begin{aligned}(AB)'+(AC)'+A'B'C' &= A'+B'+A'+C'+A'B'C' && \text{DeMorgan} \\[3pt] &= A'+B'+C' && \text{\(A'+A'=A'\), absorption} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(ABC)'}\end{aligned}\]
Report 3Lec 6 report sheet

Convert the standard SOP expressions to standard POS expressions: (a) \(\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C+\overline ABC+A\overline B\,\overline C+AB\overline C\) (b) \(\overline A\,\overline BC+\overline AB\overline C+A\overline BC+ABC\) (c) \(\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C+\overline A\,\overline BC+\overline ABC+AB\overline C+ABC\)

Answer: (a) \((A+B+C')(A+B'+C)(A'+B+C')(A'+B'+C')\); (b) \((A+B+C)(A+B'+C')(A'+B+C)(A'+B'+C)\); (c) \((A+B'+C)(A'+B+C)(A'+B+C')\)

Solution on paper

(a) \(F = A'B'C'+A'BC+AB'C'+ABC'\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Sigma(0, 3, 4, 6) && \text{number each product: unprimed = 1, primed = 0} \\[3pt] &= \Pi(1, 2, 5, 7) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(A+B+C')(A+B'+C)(A'+B+C')(A'+B'+C')} && \text{maxterm: prime where the bit is 1}\end{aligned}\]

(b) \(F = A'B'C+A'BC'+AB'C+ABC\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Sigma(1, 2, 5, 7) && \text{number each product: unprimed = 1, primed = 0} \\[3pt] &= \Pi(0, 3, 4, 6) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(A+B+C)(A+B'+C')(A'+B+C)(A'+B'+C)} && \text{maxterm: prime where the bit is 1}\end{aligned}\]

(c) \(F = A'B'C'+A'B'C+A'BC+ABC'+ABC\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Sigma(0, 1, 3, 6, 7) && \text{number each product: unprimed = 1, primed = 0} \\[3pt] &= \Pi(2, 4, 5) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{(A+B'+C)(A'+B+C)(A'+B+C')} && \text{maxterm: prime where the bit is 1}\end{aligned}\]
Report 4Lec 6 report sheet

Convert the standard POS expressions to standard SOP expressions: (a) \((A+B+C)(A+\overline B+\overline C)(\overline A+B+\overline C)(\overline A+\overline B+C)\) (b) \((A+\overline B+C)(\overline A+B+C)(\overline A+B+\overline C)(A+B+C)\) (c) \((A+B+\overline C)(A+\overline B+C)(\overline A+B+\overline C)(\overline A+\overline B+C)(\overline A+\overline B+\overline C)\)

Answer: (a) \(A'B'C+A'BC'+AB'C'+ABC\); (b) \(A'B'C+A'BC+ABC'+ABC\); (c) \(A'B'C'+A'BC+AB'C'\)

Solution on paper

(a) \(F = (A+B+C)(A+B'+C')(A'+B+C')(A'+B'+C)\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Pi(0, 3, 5, 6) && \text{number each sum: unprimed = 0, primed = 1} \\[3pt] &= \Sigma(1, 2, 4, 7) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'B'C+A'BC'+AB'C'+ABC}\end{aligned}\]

(b) \(F = (A+B'+C)(A'+B+C)(A'+B+C')(A+B+C)\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Pi(0, 2, 4, 5) && \text{number each sum: unprimed = 0, primed = 1} \\[3pt] &= \Sigma(1, 3, 6, 7) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'B'C+A'BC+ABC'+ABC}\end{aligned}\]

(c) \(F = (A+B+C')(A+B'+C)(A'+B+C')(A'+B'+C)(A'+B'+C')\)

\[\begin{aligned}F &= \Pi(1, 2, 5, 6, 7) && \text{number each sum: unprimed = 0, primed = 1} \\[3pt] &= \Sigma(0, 3, 4) && \text{missing numbers} \\[3pt] &= \boxed{A'B'C'+A'BC+AB'C'}\end{aligned}\]
Report 5Lec 6 report sheet

Derive the truth table of the standard SOP expressions: (a) \(\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C+\overline AB\overline C+A\overline B\,\overline C+AB\overline C+ABC\) (b) \(AB\overline C+\overline ABC+A\overline BC+ABC\) (c) \(\overline A\,\overline B\,\overline C+\overline AB\overline C+A\overline BC+AB\overline C+ABC\)

Answer: (a) \(\Sigma(0,2,4,6,7)\) (b) \(\Sigma(3,5,6,7)\) (c) \(\Sigma(0,2,5,6,7)\)

Solution on paper

Each product term is one minterm. Put a 1 in its row and 0 in all other rows.

\(A\)\(B\)\(C\)(a) F(b) F(c) F
000101
001000
010101
011010
100100
101011
110111
111111

Shorter forms, for checking: (a) \(C' + AB\); (b) \(BC + AC + AB\), the majority function; (c) \(A'C' + AC + BC'\).

The most recent paper was set for 2nd-year Electrical Engineering. It kept the multiple-choice bank almost unchanged and added two written questions with working space. Both written questions are lecture examples with other numbers: BCD addition and 10's-complement subtraction (Lecture 2, slides 8 and 9) and the consensus function (Lecture 3, Example 2.1) followed by a NAND–NAND circuit (Lecture 6, Example 3.10).

Total MCQs counted33334631143

Counts are multiple-choice questions only, one topic per question, counted by the same code that builds the solved papers. The written questions add: BCD arithmetic (2025), simplify + minterms + NAND circuit (2020 and 2025).

Theory or calculation?

Almost no question asks for a definition in words. About one third are recall of a fact from a slide (94 printing characters, “less than 7 variables”, NOR–NOR = OR–AND, the switch picture of a NOR gate, which forms are degenerate). The rest need a 10 to 40 second calculation: a conversion, a complement, a Gray or BCD code, DeMorgan on one expression, reading a small circuit, counting minterms or gates.

Questions that keep coming back

Question (start)WherePapers
The output of a logic gate is 1 when all its inputs are at logic 0. The gate is either 2025 Q29, 2023 Q11, 2021 Q9, 2020 Q104
The NOR-NOR realization is equivalent to: 2025 Q10, 2023 Q28, 2020 Q143
What is the minimum number of NAND gates required to implement the function after simplification, assuming that a variable and its complement are not …2025 Q31, 2023 Q13, 2021 Q163
Simplify the expression \(\overline{\overline{AB}+C}\) using DeMorgan's theorems.2023 Q40, 2021 Q10, 2020 Q113

Questions whose wording starts the same way in at least three of the four papers. Many more appear in two papers; the 2025 paper marks each repeat.

What the handwritten answers show

What earns the mark, judging from the keys and the 2023 working
  • For a circuit question: write the output of each gate next to it, then simplify (2023 Q42, Q45, Q47 are solved this way).
  • For BCD: write the 4-bit groups under each other, add, then write the +0110 row and the final groups with the decimal digit under each (Lecture 2 layout).
  • For a don't-care K-map: draw the map with X in the don't-care squares and circle the groups; several answers can be right at once, and the option “a & b & c” exists for that.
  • For NAND / NOR gate counting: simplify first, then count inverters as gates when complements are “not available”.
  • For Σ ↔ Π: write the binary pattern of each term before converting it to a number.
Mistakes found in the papers and in the handwritten answers
  • 2020 Q29: the key marks “9 binary bits” for 643; ten are needed (the 2021 key has it right).
  • 2023 Q23 and 2025 Q6: no option equals the true 8's complement \((0.4511)_8\); the expected option is 0.5621.
  • 2023 Q21: the function is printed as Σ(2, 3, 9, 11, 15) but must be Σ(1, 3, 9, 11, 15).
  • 2023 Q18 and 2025 Q3: “−9 as a signed binary number” has three valid forms among the options.
  • 2023 handwritten working: “\((394)_8\)” for 344, and a sign slip in the last line of Q45.
